Context and structure elements
  • Scope and content: This collection contains reports, schedules, minutes, budgets, requirements,
      correspondence, drawings, reviews, project plans, operational and requirements handbooks as
      related to the development, launch, and continued flight of Voyager 1 and 2, formerly the Grand
      Tour, Mariner 7 and Mariner Jupiter Saturn 1977, ranging from 1966-1990.
  • System of arrangement: This collection is arranged into nineteen series: 1. Project Records; 2.
      Mariner Jupiter Saturn 1977; 3. Voyager Interstellar Record; 4. Space Flight Operation; 5. Sun
      Sensor and Canopus Tracker; 6. Sun Sensor Quality Assurance Testing Records; 7. Guidance and
      Control; 8. Mission Telemetry Subsystem; 9. Radiation Testing of Voyager Electrical Parts; 10.
      Voyager Propulsion Module Heat Sensor Testing; 11. Voyager 1 Post-Saturn Encounter Sequence
      of Events; 12. Space Flight Team Documents; 13. Voyager Spacecraft Strategy and Operations; 14.
      Mariner Jupiter Saturn 1977 Presentations; 15. Mariner 7 Pre-Encounter Anomaly; 16. Voyager
      Computer Command Subsystem (CCS) Documents; 17. Mariner Jupiter Saturn 1977 Pre-Assembly
      Requirements; 18. Public Affairs Office; 19. Grand Tour Definition Study, Volume 1, Mission
      Analysis Document.

Notes element
   • General note(s):
          o Historical Note: The Voyager mission (previously known as Mariner Jupiter Saturn 1977) was
            a NASA-sponsored flight project. The twin Voyager spacecraft, over the course of a dozen
            years, drew back the curtain on nearly half of the solar system. From launch in 1977 through
            the spectacular parting shots of Neptune at the outer reaches of the solar system in 1989,
            this pair of spacecraft explored four planets: Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, and Neptune, as well as
            dozens of moons, and the rings and magnetic environments of those planetary systems.
            The Voyagers were designed to take advantage of a rare alignment of the outer planets that
            occurs only once every 176 years. The configuration allows a single spacecraft to swing by
            all four gas giants receiving a ‘gravity assist,’ thereby eliminating the need for large on-board
            propulsion systems. The flyby of each planet both accelerates the spacecraft and bends its
            flight path. Without these gravity assists, the flight time to Neptune would have been 30
            years.
            The second of the two Voyager spacecraft, Voyager 2, was launched first, on 1977-08-20. It
            was followed on 1977-09-05 by Voyager 1, which had a faster, shorter trajectory to Jupiter.
            Both launches took place from the Cape Canaveral Air Force Station in Florida. The Voyager
            mission makes up the longest, furthest, and most complex of any unmanned mission to this
            point.
   • Specialized note(s):
          o Conservation: Standard preparations of documents for long term storage were completed.
          o Processing information: No processing accommodations performed for this collection.
